\textstyle{\nabla_{\lambda}\star\mathcal{I}^{\mu}_{*}\star\mathcal{I}^{-w_{0}(\mu)}_{*}\star\mathcal{IC}^{\frac{\infty}{2}}_{\nu}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
Prop.
6.5
\textstyle{\mathcal{N}_{\lambda}\star\Delta_{w_{0}t_{\nu+2\zeta}}^{\frac{\infty}{2}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
(
6.13
)
\textstyle{\nabla_{\lambda}\star\mathcal{I}^{\mu}_{*}\star\mathcal{IC}^{\frac{\infty}{2}}_{\nu-\mu}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Delta_{w_{0}t_{\nu+2\zeta+w_{0}(\lambda)}}^{\frac{\infty}{2}}}
\textstyle{\nabla_{\lambda+\mu}\star\mathcal{IC}^{\frac{\infty}{2}}_{\nu-\mu}.\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

Theorem 6.9
.

[
ABB+05
, Theorem 5.3.2]

For any
w\in W^{\mathrm{res}}_{\mathrm{ext}}
and
\nu\in\mathbf{Y}
, we have

\mathrm{L}_{w}\star\mathcal{IC}^{\frac{\infty}{2}}_{\nu}\simeq\mathrm{IC}^{\frac{\infty}{2}}_{wt_{\nu}}.

Proof.

By definition we have

\mathrm{L}_{w}\star\mathcal{IC}^{\frac{\infty}{2}}_{\nu}:=(\overset{\leftarrow}{h^{\prime}})_{*}(\mathrm{sw}^{*}(\mathrm{L}_{w})\widetilde{\boxtimes}\mathcal{IC}^{\frac{\infty}{2}}_{\nu}),

where
\overset{\leftarrow}{h^{\prime}}:{{}^{I,0}\mathcal{H}_{G,N^{-}}}\to{{}^{I}_{\infty}\overline{\mathrm{Bun}}_{N^{-}}}
. Denote by
X
(resp.
Y
) the closed substack of
{{}^{I,0}\mathcal{H}_{G,N^{-}}}
(resp. of
{{}^{I}_{\infty}\overline{\mathrm{Bun}}_{N^{-}}}
) defined as the support of
\mathrm{sw}^{*}(\mathrm{L}_{w})\widetilde{\boxtimes}\mathcal{IC}^{\frac{\infty}{2}}_{\nu}
(resp. of
\mathrm{IC}^{\frac{\infty}{2}}_{wt_{\nu}}
). Consider the stratifications

X=\bigsqcup_{(u,\mu)\in W_{\mathrm{ext}}\times\mathbf{Y}}\mathbb{O}_{u}\times^{N^{-}[[t]]}{{}^{I}_{\mu}\mathcal{N}},\quad Y=\bigsqcup_{vt_{\lambda}\in W_{\mathrm{ext}}}{{}^{I}_{vt_{\lambda}}\overline{\mathrm{Bun}}_{N^{-}}},

where
\mathbb{O}_{u}\subset\mathrm{Fl}
denotes the
G[[t]]
-orbit associated with an element
u\in W_{\mathrm{ext}}
. By the proof of
[
ABB+05
, Theorem 5.3.2]
, the morphism

\overset{\leftarrow}{h^{\prime}}|_{\overset{\leftarrow}{h^{\prime}}^{-1}(Y)}:X\to Y

is stratified small with respect to the dense open subset
{{}^{I}_{wt_{\nu}}\overline{\mathrm{Bun}}_{N^{-}}}\subset Y
. Since
\overset{\leftarrow}{h^{\prime}}
is moreover proper and
\mathrm{sw}^{*}(\mathrm{L}_{w})\widetilde{\boxtimes}\mathcal{IC}^{\frac{\infty}{2}}_{\nu}
coincides with the IC-sheaf on
X
, we deduce that
\mathrm{L}_{w}\star\mathcal{IC}^{\frac{\infty}{2}}_{\nu}
coincides with the IC-sheaf on
Y
(see for instance
[
Ach21
, Proposition 3.8.10]
).
∎

7.
Iwahori-Whittaker objects

7.1.
Iwahori-Whittaker for the semi-infinite flag variety

For any
\mathbb{F}
-stack locally of finite type
\mathcal{X}
acted on by
N^{-}
, the category
\mathrm{D}_{(N^{-},\psi)}(\mathcal{X})
is defined as the full subcategory of
\mathrm{D}(\mathcal{X})
consisting of objects
\mathcal{F}
such that there exists an isomorphism
a^{*}\mathcal{F}\simeq\psi^{*}\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{AS}}\boxtimes\mathcal{F}
, where
a:N^{-}\times\mathcal{X}\to\mathcal{X}
is the morphism defining the action (and recall that
\psi:N^{-}\to\mathbb{G}_{\mathrm{a}}
was defined in §
3.6
). This category is endowed with a natural perverse
t
-structure, whose heart will be denoted by
\mathrm{Perv}_{(N^{-},\psi)}(\mathcal{X})
, and which is sent to
\mathrm{Perv}(\mathcal{X})
by the forgetful functor
\mathrm{For}_{(N^{-},\psi)}:\mathrm{D}_{(N^{-},\psi)}(\mathcal{X})\to\mathrm{D}(\mathcal{X})
.

First consider the category
\mathrm{Perv}_{(N^{-},\psi)}(G/B^{-})
. It is a classical fact that the only
N^{-}
-orbit supporting a non-zero Whittaker local system is the open dense orbit, i.e. the orbit associated with
w_{0}
. Thus, the category
\mathrm{Perv}_{(N^{-},\psi)}(G/B^{-})
contains a unique simple object, denoted by
\psi_{G/B^{-}}
, defined as the Goresky-Macpherson extension of the rank-one Whittaker local system on this orbit. Next, we define the category
\mathrm{Perv}_{(I^{-}_{\mathrm{u}},\psi)}(\mathcal{F}l^{\frac{\infty}{2}})
as the full subcategory of
\mathrm{Perv}_{(N^{-},\psi)}({{}_{\infty}^{1}\overline{\mathrm{Bun}}_{N^{-}}})
consisting of
T
-equivariant perverse sheaves satisfying the factorization and equivariance conditions (cf. §
5.1
). The same arguments as the one used in the proof of Proposition
5.4
show that the simple objects of
\mathrm{Perv}_{(I^{-}_{\mathrm{u}},\psi)}(\mathcal{F}l^{\frac{\infty}{2}})
are of the form

\mathrm{IC}_{\nu}^{\psi}:=(\overline{i}_{\nu})_{!*}\left(\psi_{G/B^{-}}\widetilde{\boxtimes}\mathrm{IC}_{{{}_{\nu}\overline{\mathrm{Bun}}_{N^{-}}}}\right),~\nu\in\mathbf{Y}.

We have the convolution functor

\mathrm{Perv}_{\mathcal{IW}}(\mathrm{Gr})\times\mathrm{Perv}_{G[[t]]}(\mathcal{F}l^{\frac{\infty}{2}})\to\mathrm{Perv}_{(I^{-}_{\mathrm{u}},\psi)}(\mathcal{F}l^{\frac{\infty}{2}}).

Recall (§
2.1
) that we have picked
\zeta\in\mathbf{Y}
such that
\langle\alpha,\zeta\rangle=1
for all
\alpha\in\mathfrak{R}_{s}
. The proof of the next result is the same as that of Theorem
6.9
.

Proposition 7.1
.

[
ABB+05
, Theorem 5.3.5]

For any
\nu\in\mathbf{Y}
, we have

\mathrm{L}^{\mathcal{IW}}_{\zeta}\star\mathcal{IC}^{\frac{\infty}{2}}_{\nu}\simeq\mathrm{IC}^{\psi}_{\nu+\zeta}.

For any
\nu\in\mathbf{Y}
, we have the corresponding standard and costandard objects

\nabla_{\nu}^{\psi}:=(\overline{i}_{\nu})_{*}\left(\psi_{G/B^{-}}\widetilde{\boxtimes}\mathrm{IC}_{{{}_{\nu}\overline{\mathrm{Bun}}_{N^{-}}}}\right),\quad\Delta_{\nu}^{\psi}:=(\overline{i}_{\nu})_{!}\left(\psi_{G/B^{-}}\widetilde{\boxtimes}\mathrm{IC}_{{{}_{\nu}\overline{\mathrm{Bun}}_{N^{-}}}}\right).

By the proof of
[
ABB+05
, Corollary 4.4.5]
, we know that the inclusion of
{{}_{\nu}^{I_{\mathrm{u}}}\overline{\mathrm{Bun}}_{N^{-}}}
into
{{}_{\leq\nu}^{I_{\mathrm{u}}}\overline{\mathrm{Bun}}_{N^{-}}}
is affine, so that both objects above are perverse sheaves. They are then easily seen to be objects of
\mathrm{Perv}_{(I^{-}_{\mathrm{u}},\psi)}(\mathcal{F}l^{\frac{\infty}{2}})
.

Theorem 7.2
.

[
ABB+05
, Theorem 4.4.10]

For any
\nu\in\mathbf{Y}
, the canonical morphisms

\Delta_{\nu}^{\psi}\to\mathrm{IC}_{\nu}^{\psi}\to\nabla_{\nu}^{\psi}

are isomorphisms.

Proof.

By the arguments of
loc. cit.
, we are reduced to showing that the Euler characteristic of the stalk of
\mathrm{IC}_{\nu+\zeta}^{\psi}
at any point of the locally closed substack
\mathrm{ev}_{\nu^{\prime}}^{-1}(N^{-}\cdot w_{0})
, for
\nu^{\prime}<\nu+\zeta
, is zero. By Proposition
7.1
, this is equivalent to computing the stalk of
\mathrm{L}^{\mathcal{IW}}_{\zeta}\star\mathcal{IC}^{\frac{\infty}{2}}_{\nu}
. Using similar arguments as in the proof of Proposition
6.5
, this stalk identifies with the extension of a finite number of certain complexes of
\mathbb{k}
-vector spaces
K_{\nu^{\prime\prime}}
,
\nu^{\prime\prime}<\nu
. Each
K_{\nu^{\prime\prime}}
is defined as the stalk of the direct image under

\overset{\leftarrow}{h^{\prime}}:(\overset{\leftarrow}{h^{\prime}})^{-1}({{}^{1}_{\nu^{\prime}}\overline{\mathrm{Bun}}}_{N^{-}})\bigcap(\overset{\rightarrow}{h^{\prime}})^{-1}({{}_{\nu^{\prime\prime}}\overline{\mathrm{Bun}}}_{N^{-}})\bigcap{{}^{1,0}\overline{\mathcal{H}}^{\zeta}_{G,N^{-}}}\to{{}^{1}_{\nu^{\prime}}\overline{\mathrm{Bun}}}_{N^{-}}

of the restriction of
\mathrm{L}^{\mathcal{IW}}_{\zeta}\widetilde{\boxtimes}\mathcal{IC}^{\frac{\infty}{2}}_{\nu}
to the above locally closed substack. Moreover, it follows from the constructions that the projection
\overset{\rightarrow}{h^{\prime}}
identifies that same locally closed substack with

\left(N((t))\cdot w_{0}(\nu^{\prime}-\nu^{\prime\prime})\cap I^{-}\cdot(w_{0}(\zeta))\right)\times^{N^{-}[[t]]}{{}_{\nu^{\prime\prime}}{\mathcal{N}}}.

Therefore

\displaystyle K_{\nu^{\prime\prime}}

\displaystyle\simeq H^{\bullet}_{c}(N((t))\cdot w_{0}(\nu^{\prime}-\nu^{\prime\prime})\cap I^{-}\cdot(w_{0}(\zeta)),\mathcal{L}\otimes_{\mathbb{k}}\mathcal{IC}^{\frac{\infty}{2}}_{\nu}|_{{{}_{\nu^{\prime\prime}}\mathrm{Bun}}_{N^{-}}})

\displaystyle\simeq H^{\bullet}_{c}(N((t))\cdot w_{0}(\nu^{\prime}-\nu^{\prime\prime})\cap I^{-}\cdot(w_{0}(\zeta)),\mathcal{L})\otimes_{\mathbb{k}}\mathcal{IC}^{\frac{\infty}{2}}_{\nu}|_{{{}_{\nu^{\prime\prime}}\mathrm{Bun}}_{N^{-}}}

where
\mathcal{L}
denotes the rank-one Iwahori-Whittaker local system on
I^{-}\cdot(w_{0}(\zeta))
, and
\mathcal{IC}^{\frac{\infty}{2}}_{\nu}|_{{{}_{\nu^{\prime\prime}}\mathrm{Bun}}_{N^{-}}}
is meant to be the stalk of
\mathcal{IC}^{\frac{\infty}{2}}_{\nu}
at any point of
{{}_{\nu^{\prime\prime}}\mathrm{Bun}}_{N^{-}}
.

On the other hand, let us put
\mu_{r}:=(\ell^{r}-1)\zeta
for any integer
r\geq 0
. The canonical morphisms

\mathcal{I}^{\mu_{r}}_{!}\to\mathcal{IC}^{\mu_{r}}\to\mathcal{I}^{\mu_{r}}_{*}

are isomorphisms for any
r
thanks to Lemma
3.7
, so that (by applying the functor
\Delta^{\mathcal{IW}}_{\zeta}\star(-)
) the canonical morphisms

\Delta^{\mathcal{IW}}_{\mu_{r}+\zeta}\to\mathrm{L}_{\mu_{r}+\zeta}^{\mathcal{IW}}\to\nabla^{\mathcal{IW}}_{\mu_{r}+\zeta}

are also isomorphisms. In particular, the IC-sheaf
\mathrm{L}_{\mu_{r}+\zeta}^{\mathcal{IW}}\in\mathrm{Perv}_{\mathcal{IW}}(\mathrm{Gr})
is clean.

Notice that the map

f:\nu^{\prime\prime}\mapsto\mu_{r}-(\nu-\nu^{\prime\prime})

induces a bijection between the sets
\{\nu^{\prime\prime},~\nu^{\prime\prime}\leq\nu\}
and
\{\mu^{\prime\prime},~\mu^{\prime\prime}\leq\mu_{r}\}
.
Put
\mu^{\prime}_{r}:=f(\nu^{\prime})
. The stalk of
\mathrm{L}_{\mu_{r}+\zeta}^{\mathcal{IW}}\simeq\mathrm{L}_{\zeta}^{\mathcal{IW}}\star\mathcal{IC}^{\mu_{r}}
at
[t^{\mu^{\prime}_{r}}]\in Y_{\mu^{\prime}_{r}}
can be written as an extension of certain complexes
\widetilde{K}_{\mu^{\prime\prime}}
, for
\mu^{\prime\prime}\leq\mu_{r}
(only a finite number of complexes will be non-zero). Each
\widetilde{K}_{\mu^{\prime\prime}}
is defined as the stalk at
[t^{\mu^{\prime}_{r}}]
of the direct image under the multiplication morphism

(7.1)

m:I^{-}\cdot(w_{0}(\zeta))\times^{G((t))}\mathrm{Gr}^{\mu^{\prime\prime}}\to\mathrm{Gr}

of the restriction of
\mathrm{L}_{\zeta}^{\mathcal{IW}}\widetilde{\boxtimes}\mathcal{IC}^{\mu_{r}}
to
I^{-}\cdot(w_{0}\zeta)\times^{G((t))}\mathrm{Gr}^{\mu^{\prime\prime}}
. By
[
ABB+05
, Remark 5.3.3]
, the preimage of
[t^{\mu^{\prime}_{r}}]
by the morphism (
7.1
) is isomorphic to
N((t))\cdot w_{0}(\mu^{\prime}_{r}-\mu^{\prime\prime})\cap I^{-}\cdot(w_{0}(\zeta))
when
r
is large enough (so that
\mu_{r}
is dominant enough). Thus

\displaystyle\widetilde{K}_{\mu^{\prime\prime}}

\displaystyle\simeq H^{\bullet}_{c}(N((t))\cdot w_{0}(\mu^{\prime}_{r}-\mu^{\prime\prime})\cap I^{-}\cdot(w_{0}(\zeta)),\mathcal{L}\otimes_{\mathbb{k}}\mathcal{IC}^{\mu_{r}}|_{\mathrm{Gr}^{\mu^{\prime\prime}}})

\displaystyle\simeq H^{\bullet}_{c}(N((t))\cdot w_{0}(\mu^{\prime}_{r}-\mu^{\prime\prime})\cap I^{-}\cdot(w_{0}(\zeta)),\mathcal{L})\otimes_{\mathbb{k}}\mathcal{IC}^{\mu_{r}}|_{\mathrm{Gr}^{\mu^{\prime\prime}}}

where
\mathcal{IC}^{\mu_{r}}|_{\mathrm{Gr}^{\mu^{\prime\prime}}}
is meant to be the stalk of
\mathcal{IC}^{\mu_{r}}
at any point of
\mathrm{Gr}^{\mu^{\prime\prime}}
.

Since
\mu_{r}-f(\nu^{\prime\prime})=\nu-\nu^{\prime\prime}
by construction, we can take
r
large enough so that

\mathcal{IC}^{\frac{\infty}{2}}_{\nu}|_{{{}_{\nu^{\prime\prime}}\mathrm{Bun}}_{N^{-}}}\simeq\mathcal{IC}^{\mu_{r}}|_{\mathrm{Gr}^{f(\nu^{\prime\prime})}}

for all
\nu^{\prime\prime}<\nu
such that
K_{\nu^{\prime\prime}}
is non-zero by Corollary
5.15
. Moreover
\mu^{\prime}_{r}-f(\nu^{\prime\prime})=\nu^{\prime}-\nu^{\prime\prime}
by definition of
f
. So we get an isomorphism

K_{\nu^{\prime\prime}}\simeq\widetilde{K}_{f(\nu^{\prime\prime})}\quad\forall\nu^{\prime\prime}\leq\nu.

So the Euler characteristic of the stalk of
\mathrm{IC}_{\nu+\zeta}^{\psi}
at any point of
\mathrm{ev}_{\nu^{\prime}}^{-1}(N^{-}\cdot w_{0})
coincides with the Euler characteristic of the stalk of
\mathrm{L}_{\mu_{r}+\zeta}^{\mathcal{IW}}
at
[t^{\mu^{\prime}_{r}}]
. But
\mu^{\prime}_{r}<\mu_{r}+\zeta
, so that the latter stalk is zero by cleanness. This concludes the proof.
∎

7.2.
Partial integrability and consequences

We denote by

(7.2)

\mathrm{av}_{!,(N^{-},\psi)},~\mathrm{av}_{*,(N^{-},\psi)}:\mathrm{D}({{}_{\infty}^{1}\overline{\mathrm{Bun}}}_{N^{-}})\to\mathrm{D}_{(I^{-}_{\mathrm{u}},\psi)}({{}_{\infty}^{1}\overline{\mathrm{Bun}}}_{N^{-}})

the functors which are respectively left and right adjoint to the forgetful functor. They are defined by

\mathrm{av}_{!,(N^{-},\psi)}(\mathcal{F}):=a_{!}(\psi^{*}\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{AS}}\boxtimes\mathcal{F})[2\mathrm{dim}(N^{-})],\quad\mathrm{av}_{*,(N^{-},\psi)}(\mathcal{F}):=a_{*}(\psi^{*}\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{AS}}\boxtimes\mathcal{F}),

where
a:N^{-}\times{{}_{\infty}^{1}\overline{\mathrm{Bun}}}_{N^{-}}\to{{}_{\infty}^{1}\overline{\mathrm{Bun}}}_{N^{-}}
is the action morphism.
Moreover we denote by

\mathrm{Av}_{!,(I_{\mathrm{u}},\psi)},~\mathrm{Av}_{*,(I_{\mathrm{u}},\psi)}:\mathrm{D}({{}_{\infty}^{I_{\mathrm{u}}}\overline{\mathrm{Bun}}}_{N^{-}})\to\mathrm{D}_{(I^{-}_{\mathrm{u}},\psi)}({{}_{\infty}^{1}\overline{\mathrm{Bun}}}_{N^{-}})

the functors obtained by composing (
7.2
) with the forgetful functor
\mathrm{D}({{}_{\infty}^{I_{\mathrm{u}}}\overline{\mathrm{Bun}}}_{N^{-}})\to\mathrm{D}({{}_{\infty}^{1}\overline{\mathrm{Bun}}}_{N^{-}})
.

Let us denote by

\mathrm{For}^{I}_{I_{\mathrm{u}}}:\mathrm{D}({{}_{\infty}^{I}\overline{\mathrm{Bun}}}_{N^{-}})\to\mathrm{D}({{}_{\infty}^{I_{\mathrm{u}}}\overline{\mathrm{Bun}}}_{N^{-}})

the forgetful functor. From the definitions, we have isomorphisms of functors from
\mathrm{D}({{}_{\infty}^{I}\overline{\mathrm{Bun}}}_{N^{-}})
to
\mathrm{D}_{(I^{-}_{\mathrm{u}},\psi)}({{}_{\infty}^{1}\overline{\mathrm{Bun}}}_{N^{-}})
:

(7.3)

\mathrm{Av}_{!,(I_{\mathrm{u}},\psi)}[-2\mathrm{dim}(N^{-})]\circ\mathrm{For}^{I}_{I_{\mathrm{u}}}\simeq\Delta_{\psi}\star(-),\quad\mathrm{Av}_{*,(I_{\mathrm{u}},\psi)}\circ\mathrm{For}^{I}_{I_{\mathrm{u}}}\simeq\nabla_{\psi}\star(-)

where
\Delta_{\psi}\in\mathrm{Perv}_{(N^{-},\psi)}(G/B)
(resp.
\nabla_{\psi}
) is the
!
-extension (resp.
*
-extension) of the rank one Whittaker local system on the dense
N^{-}
-orbit in
G/B
(and the convolution product
\star
is defined as in Remark
5.20
).

Lemma 7.3
.

[
ABB+05
, Lemma 4.4.12]

The canonical morphism of functors

\mathrm{Av}_{!,(I_{\mathrm{u}},\psi)}[-\mathrm{dim}(N^{-})]\to\mathrm{Av}_{*,(I_{\mathrm{u}},\psi)}[\mathrm{dim}(N^{-})]

is an isomorphism. Moreover, the resulting functor

\mathrm{D}({{}_{\infty}^{I_{\mathrm{u}}}\overline{\mathrm{Bun}}}_{N^{-}})\to\mathrm{D}_{(I^{-}_{\mathrm{u}},\psi)}({{}_{\infty}^{1}\overline{\mathrm{Bun}}}_{N^{-}})

is
t
-exact.

We will denote by

\mathrm{Av}_{(I_{\mathrm{u}},\psi)}:\mathrm{D}({{}_{\infty}^{I_{\mathrm{u}}}\overline{\mathrm{Bun}}}_{N^{-}})\to\mathrm{D}_{(I^{-}_{\mathrm{u}},\psi)}({{}_{\infty}^{1}\overline{\mathrm{Bun}}}_{N^{-}})

the obtained
t
-exact functor.

Proof.

First, notice that the category
\mathrm{D}({{}_{\infty}^{I_{\mathrm{u}}}\overline{\mathrm{Bun}}}_{N^{-}})
is generated (as a triangulated category) by the essential image of the forgetful functor

\mathrm{For}^{I}_{I_{\mathrm{u}}}:\mathrm{D}({{}_{\infty}^{I}\overline{\mathrm{Bun}}}_{N^{-}})\to\mathrm{D}({{}_{\infty}^{I_{\mathrm{u}}}\overline{\mathrm{Bun}}}_{N^{-}}).

Thus, the first point can be proved for the corresponding functors composed with
\mathrm{For}^{I}_{I_{\mathrm{u}}}
. In this case, we have the isomorphisms of functors (
7.3
), so that the morphism

\mathrm{Av}_{!,(I_{\mathrm{u}},\psi)}[-\mathrm{dim}(N^{-})]\circ\mathrm{For}^{I}_{I_{\mathrm{u}}}\to\mathrm{Av}_{*,(I_{\mathrm{u}},\psi)}[\mathrm{dim}(N^{-})]\circ\mathrm{For}^{I}_{I_{\mathrm{u}}}

is induced by the canonical morphism
\Delta_{\psi}\to\nabla_{\psi}
. But the latter morphism is an isomorphism, as both objects are easily seen to be isomorphic to the simple perverse sheaf
\psi_{G/B}
.

Finally, we deduce that the resulting functor is
t
-exact. Indeed,
\mathrm{av}_{*,(N^{-},\psi)}
is right
t
-exact since it is defined as the
*
-pushforward along an affine morphism
[
BBDG18
, Théorème 4.1.1]
, and
\mathrm{av}_{!,(N^{-},\psi)}
is left
t
-exact since it is defined as the
!
-pushforward along an affine morphism
[
BBDG18
, Corollaire 4.1.2]
.
∎

An object of
\mathrm{Perv}_{I_{\mathrm{u}}}(\mathcal{F}l^{\frac{\infty}{2}})
is called
partially integrable
if all of its irreducible sub-quotients are of the form
\mathrm{IC}^{\frac{\infty}{2}}_{wt_{\nu}}
, with
\nu\in\mathbf{Y}
and
w\neq w_{0}
. We let
{}^{f}\mathrm{Perv}_{I_{\mathrm{u}}}(\mathcal{F}l^{\frac{\infty}{2}})
denote the resulting Serre quotient category obtained by quotienting
\mathrm{Perv}_{I_{\mathrm{u}}}(\mathcal{F}l^{\frac{\infty}{2}})
by the sub-category of partially integrable objects. The next result parallels Proposition
3.26
.

Proposition 7.4
.

For any
wt_{\nu}\in W_{\mathrm{ext}}
we have isomorphisms

\mathrm{Av}_{I_{\mathrm{u}},\psi}(\nabla^{\frac{\infty}{2}}_{wt_{\nu}})\simeq\nabla^{\psi}_{\nu},\quad\mathrm{Av}_{I_{\mathrm{u}},\psi}(\Delta^{\frac{\infty}{2}}_{wt_{\nu}})\simeq\Delta^{\psi}_{\nu}.

Proof.

By (
7.3
), we have

\mathrm{Av}_{I_{\mathrm{u}},\psi}(\nabla^{\frac{\infty}{2}}_{wt_{\nu}})\simeq\nabla_{\psi}\star\nabla^{\frac{\infty}{2}}_{wt_{\nu}},

where
\nabla^{\frac{\infty}{2}}_{wt_{\nu}}
is seen as an object of
\mathrm{D}({{}_{\infty}^{I}\overline{\mathrm{Bun}}}_{N^{-}})
. Recall that, by definition

\nabla^{\frac{\infty}{2}}_{wt_{\nu}}=(\overline{i}_{\nu})_{*}\left(\tilde{\nabla}_{w}\widetilde{\boxtimes}\mathrm{IC}_{{{}_{\nu}\overline{\mathrm{Bun}}}_{N^{-}}}\right).

So one can check that

\nabla_{\psi}\star\nabla^{\frac{\infty}{2}}_{wt_{\nu}}\simeq(\overline{i}_{\nu})_{*}\left((\nabla_{\psi}\star\tilde{\nabla}_{w})\widetilde{\boxtimes}\mathrm{IC}_{{{}_{\nu}\overline{\mathrm{Bun}}}_{N^{-}}}\right).

As in
[
BY13
, Lemma 4.4.8]
, we have an isomorphism
\nabla_{\psi}\star\tilde{\nabla}_{w}\simeq\psi_{G/B}
, concluding the proof of the first isomorphism. The proof of the second one is similar.
∎

The proof of the next result is done exactly as in
[
ABB+05
, Proposition 4.4.13]
.

Proposition 7.5
.

(1)

The functor

\mathrm{Av}_{(I_{\mathrm{u}},\psi)}:\mathrm{Perv}_{I_{\mathrm{u}}}(\mathcal{F}l^{\frac{\infty}{2}})\to\mathrm{Perv}_{(I^{-}_{\mathrm{u}},\psi)}(\mathcal{F}l^{\frac{\infty}{2}})

factors through
{}^{f}\mathrm{Perv}_{I_{\mathrm{u}}}(\mathcal{F}l^{\frac{\infty}{2}})
.

(2)

The resulting functor

{}^{f}\mathrm{Perv}_{I_{\mathrm{u}}}(\mathcal{F}l^{\frac{\infty}{2}})\to\mathrm{Perv}_{(I^{-}_{\mathrm{u}},\psi)}(\mathcal{F}l^{\frac{\infty}{2}})

is exact and faithful.

8.
The convolution functor

8.1.
Construction of the functor

Here we follow
[
ABB+05
, §6.1.1]
. We recall the construction of a functor

\mathrm{Conv}^{\mathrm{Hecke}}:\mathrm{Mod}^{\mathbf{Y}}_{I_{\mathrm{u}}}(\mathcal{R})\to\mathrm{Ind}(\mathrm{Perv}_{{I_{\mathrm{u}}}}(\mathcal{F}l^{\frac{\infty}{2}})),

which will (conjecturally) restrict to an equivalence on the categories of finite length objects. Let
\mathcal{S}\in\mathrm{Mod}^{\mathbf{Y}}_{I_{\mathrm{u}}}(\mathcal{R})
. For any
\lambda,\mu\in\mathbf{Y}
, consider the morphisms

\displaystyle\alpha_{\lambda,\mu}:\mathcal{S}_{\lambda}\star\mathcal{R}_{-\mu}\star\mathcal{IC}^{\frac{\infty}{2}}_{\mu-\lambda}\to\mathcal{S}_{\lambda-\mu}\star\mathcal{IC}^{\frac{\infty}{2}}_{\mu-\lambda}

\displaystyle\beta_{\lambda,\mu}:\mathcal{S}_{\lambda}\star\mathcal{R}_{-\mu}\star\mathcal{IC}^{\frac{\infty}{2}}_{\mu-\lambda}\to\mathcal{S}_{\lambda}\star\mathcal{IC}^{\frac{\infty}{2}}_{-\lambda},

induced respectively by the right
\mathcal{R}
-module structure on
\mathcal{S}
and the left
\mathcal{R}
-module structure on
\mathcal{IC}^{\frac{\infty}{2}}
(cf. §
6.6
).

We then define
\mathrm{Conv}^{\mathrm{Hecke}}(\mathcal{S})
as the coequalizer of the following diagram:

\bigoplus_{\lambda,\mu}\mathcal{S}_{\lambda}\star\mathcal{R}_{-\mu}\star\mathcal{IC}^{\frac{\infty}{2}}_{\mu-\lambda}\rightrightarrows\bigoplus_{\nu}\mathcal{S}_{\nu}\star\mathcal{IC}^{\frac{\infty}{2}}_{-\nu},

where the two arrows correspond to
\alpha_{\lambda,\mu}
and
\beta_{\lambda,\mu}
.

In other words, the object
\mathrm{Conv}^{\mathrm{Hecke}}(\mathcal{S})
represents the covariant functor which, to any
\mathcal{F}\in\mathrm{Ind}(\mathrm{Perv}_{{I_{\mathrm{u}}}}(\mathcal{F}l^{\frac{\infty}{2}}))
, associates the set of collections of morphisms
(\mathcal{S}_{\nu}\star\mathcal{IC}^{\frac{\infty}{2}}_{-\nu}\to\mathcal{F})_{\nu\in\mathbf{Y}}
such that, for any
\lambda,\mu\in\mathbf{Y}
, the following diagram commutes:

(8.1)

\mathcal{S}_{\lambda}\star\mathcal{R}_{-\mu}\star\mathcal{IC}^{\frac{\infty}{2}}_{\mu-\lambda}\rightrightarrows\mathcal{F},

where the top (resp. bottom) arrow is the composition of
\alpha_{\lambda,\mu}
(resp.
\beta_{\lambda,\mu}
) with the morphism
\mathcal{S}_{\lambda-\mu}\star\mathcal{IC}^{\frac{\infty}{2}}_{\mu-\lambda}\to\mathcal{F}
(resp.
\mathcal{S}_{\lambda}\star\mathcal{IC}^{\frac{\infty}{2}}_{-\lambda}\to\mathcal{F}
).

8.2.
First properties

Recall that
\Phi:\mathrm{Perv}_{I_{\mathrm{u}}}(\mathrm{Gr})\to\mathrm{mod}^{\mathbf{Y}}_{I_{\mathrm{u}}}(\mathcal{R})
was defined in §
3.10
.

Proposition 8.1
.

[
ABB+05
, Proposition 3.1.2]

For any
\mathcal{S}\in\mathrm{Perv}_{I_{\mathrm{u}}}(\mathrm{Gr})
and
\mu\in\mathbf{Y}
, we have a canonical isomorphism

\mathrm{Conv}^{\mathrm{Hecke}}(\Phi(\mathcal{S})\langle\mu\rangle)\simeq\mathcal{S}\star\mathcal{IC}^{\frac{\infty}{2}}_{-\mu}.

Proof.

We first construct a morphism of functors

(8.2)

\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Ind}(\mathrm{Perv}_{{I_{\mathrm{u}}}}(\mathcal{F}l^{\frac{\infty}{2}}))}\left(\mathrm{Conv}^{\mathrm{Hecke}}(\Phi(\mathcal{S})\langle\mu\rangle),-\right)\to\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Ind}(\mathrm{Perv}_{{I_{\mathrm{u}}}}(\mathcal{F}l^{\frac{\infty}{2}}))}\left(\mathcal{S}\star\mathcal{IC}^{\frac{\infty}{2}}_{-\mu},-\right).

For any morphism
\mathrm{Conv}^{\mathrm{Hecke}}(\Phi(\mathcal{S})\langle\mu\rangle)\to\mathcal{F}
, we take the component

(\Phi(\mathcal{S})\langle\mu\rangle)_{\mu}\star\mathcal{IC}^{\frac{\infty}{2}}_{-\mu}\to\mathcal{F}.

Composing with the canonical morphism
\mathcal{S}\to\mathcal{S}\star\mathcal{R}_{0}=(\Phi(\mathcal{S})\langle\mu\rangle)_{\mu}
, we obtain the desired map
\mathcal{S}\star\mathcal{IC}^{\frac{\infty}{2}}_{-\mu}\to\mathcal{F}
.

Next, we construct an inverse of
(
8.2
)
. Consider a morphism
\varphi:\mathcal{S}\star\mathcal{IC}^{\frac{\infty}{2}}_{-\mu}\to\mathcal{F}
. For any
\lambda\in\mathbf{Y}
, we construct a morphism
(\Phi(\mathcal{S})\langle\mu\rangle)_{\lambda}\star\mathcal{IC}^{\frac{\infty}{2}}_{-\lambda}\to\mathcal{F}
in the following way:

(\Phi(\mathcal{S})\langle\mu\rangle)_{\lambda}\star\mathcal{IC}^{\frac{\infty}{2}}_{-\lambda}=\mathcal{S}\star\mathcal{R}_{\lambda-\mu}\star\mathcal{IC}^{\frac{\infty}{2}}_{-\lambda}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{id}\star H_{\lambda-\mu,-\lambda}}\mathcal{S}\star\mathcal{IC}^{\frac{\infty}{2}}_{-\mu}\xrightarrow{\varphi}\mathcal{F}.

The fact that this collection of morphisms makes the diagram (
8.1
) commute follows from the associativity property of the action of
\mathcal{R}
on
\mathcal{IC}^{\frac{\infty}{2}}
(Proposition
6.6
). The fact that we indeed obtain an inverse of (
8.2
) is readily checked.
∎

Corollary 8.2
.

For any
w\in W_{\mathrm{ext}}
, we have an isomorphism

\mathrm{Conv}^{\mathrm{Hecke}}(\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{w})\simeq\mathrm{IC}^{\frac{\infty}{2}}_{w}.

Proof.

Let
w\in W_{\mathrm{ext}}
, and write it as
w=yt_{\lambda}
for
(y,\lambda)\in W^{\mathrm{res}}_{\mathrm{ext}}\times\mathbf{Y}
. By construction,
\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{w}=\Phi(\mathrm{L}_{y})\langle-\lambda\rangle
, so that Proposition
8.1
yields an isomorphism

\mathrm{Conv}^{\mathrm{Hecke}}(\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{w})\simeq\mathrm{L}_{y}\star\mathcal{IC}^{\frac{\infty}{2}}_{\lambda}.

By Theorem
6.9
, the right-hand side is isomorphic to
\mathrm{IC}^{\frac{\infty}{2}}_{yt_{\lambda}}
.
∎

Proposition 8.3
.

[
ABB+05
, Proposition 6.2.2]

The functor
\mathrm{Conv}^{\mathrm{Hecke}}
is exact.

Proof.

By construction, the functor
\mathrm{Conv}^{\mathrm{Hecke}}
is right exact. So it remains to check that it is left exact. Since
\mathrm{Mod}^{\mathbf{Y}}_{I_{\mathrm{u}}}(\mathcal{R})
is the ind-completion of
\mathrm{mod}^{\mathbf{Y}}_{I_{\mathrm{u}}}(\mathcal{R})
, it suffices to check left exactness for the restriction of
\mathrm{Conv}^{\mathrm{Hecke}}
to
\mathrm{mod}^{\mathbf{Y}}_{I_{\mathrm{u}}}(\mathcal{R})
. So let

0\to\mathcal{S}_{1}\xrightarrow{u}\mathcal{S}_{2}\to\mathcal{S}\to 0

be an exact sequence in
\mathrm{mod}^{\mathbf{Y}}_{I_{\mathrm{u}}}(\mathcal{R})
. We have to prove that
\mathrm{Conv}^{\mathrm{Hecke}}(\mathcal{S}_{1})\to\mathrm{Conv}^{\mathrm{Hecke}}(\mathcal{S}_{2})
is injective. By an inductive argument on the length of
\mathcal{S}_{2}
, it is sufficient to prove the result in the case where
\mathcal{S}
is simple. In particular (thanks to Theorem
3.15
), we may and will assume that there exist
\mathcal{F}\in\mathrm{Perv}_{I_{\mathrm{u}}}(\mathrm{Gr})
and
\mu\in\mathbf{Y}
such that
\mathcal{S}\simeq\Phi(\mathcal{F})\langle\mu\rangle
. Consider the morphism

\mathcal{F}\to\mathcal{F}\star\mathcal{R}_{0}\simeq\mathcal{S}_{-\mu}.

Since
(\mathcal{S}_{2})_{-\mu},~\mathcal{S}_{-\mu}
are ind-objects of the category
\mathrm{Perv}_{I_{\mathrm{u}}}(\mathrm{Gr})
, there exists
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}\in\mathrm{Perv}_{I_{\mathrm{u}}}(\mathrm{Gr})
together with a morphism
\varphi:\mathcal{F}^{\prime}\to(\mathcal{S}_{2})_{-\mu}
and a surjective morphism
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}\to\mathcal{F}
such that the following diagram commutes

\textstyle{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{F}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{(\mathcal{S}_{2})_{-\mu}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{S}_{-\mu}.}

In particular, we obtain a morphism
a:\Phi(\mathcal{F}^{\prime})\langle\mu\rangle\to\mathcal{S}_{2}
by Proposition
3.14
. We put
\mathcal{F}^{\prime\prime}:=\mathrm{ker}(\mathcal{F}^{\prime}\to\mathcal{F})
. We have a commutative diagram

(8.3)

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Phi(\mathcal{F}^{\prime\prime})\langle\mu\rangle\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{id}}
\textstyle{\Phi(\mathcal{F}^{\prime\prime})\langle\mu\rangle\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{S}_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{id}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{S}_{1}\oplus(\Phi(\mathcal{F}^{\prime})\langle\mu\rangle)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{u\oplus a}
\textstyle{\Phi(\mathcal{F}^{\prime})\langle\mu\rangle\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{S}_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{S}_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Phi(\mathcal{F})\langle\mu\rangle\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0.}

To prove the desired result, we only need to prove that the maps
\mathrm{Conv}^{\mathrm{Hecke}}(\mathcal{S}_{1})\to\mathrm{Conv}^{\mathrm{Hecke}}(\mathcal{S}_{1}\oplus(\Phi(\mathcal{F}^{\prime})\langle\mu\rangle))
and
\mathrm{Conv}^{\mathrm{Hecke}}(\Phi(\mathcal{F}^{\prime\prime})\langle\mu\rangle)\to\mathrm{Conv}^{\mathrm{Hecke}}(\mathcal{S}_{1}\oplus(\Phi(\mathcal{F}^{\prime})\langle\mu\rangle))
are injective. For the first one this is obvious, since
\mathrm{Conv}^{\mathrm{Hecke}}
transforms direct sums into direct sums. For the second one, it suffices to check that

\mathrm{Conv}^{\mathrm{Hecke}}(\Phi(\mathcal{F}^{\prime\prime})\langle\mu\rangle)\to\mathrm{Conv}^{\mathrm{Hecke}}(\Phi(\mathcal{F}^{\prime})\langle\mu\rangle)

is injective. But one deduces from Proposition
8.1
that the latter morphism coincides with

\mathcal{F}^{\prime\prime}\star\mathcal{IC}^{\frac{\infty}{2}}_{\mu}\to\mathcal{F}^{\prime}\star\mathcal{IC}^{\frac{\infty}{2}}_{\mu},

obtained by convolving the injection
\mathcal{F}^{\prime\prime}\to\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
with
\mathcal{IC}^{\frac{\infty}{2}}_{\mu}
. This morphism is injective thanks to exactness of convolution (Theorem
6.4
).
∎

Notice that, thanks to Proposition
8.3
and Corollary
8.2
, we immediately obtain:

Proposition 8.4
.

The functor
\mathrm{Conv}^{\mathrm{Hecke}}
sends finite length objects to finite length objects.

So restriction yields a functor

\mathrm{Conv}^{\mathrm{Hecke}}:\mathrm{mod}^{\mathbf{Y}}_{I_{\mathrm{u}}}(\mathcal{R})\to\mathrm{Perv}_{{I_{\mathrm{u}}}}(\mathcal{F}l^{\frac{\infty}{2}}).

Notice that, by replacing
\mathrm{Mod}^{\mathbf{Y}}_{I_{\mathrm{u}}}(\mathcal{R})
with
\mathrm{Mod}^{\mathbf{Y}}_{\mathcal{IW}}(\mathcal{R})
in the definition of
\mathrm{Conv}^{\mathrm{Hecke}}
, we obtain a functor

\mathrm{Conv}^{\mathrm{Hecke}}_{\mathcal{IW}}:\mathrm{Mod}^{\mathbf{Y}}_{\mathcal{IW}}(\mathcal{R})\to\mathrm{Ind}(\mathrm{Perv}_{(I^{-}_{\mathrm{u}},\psi)}(\mathcal{F}l^{\frac{\infty}{2}}))

satisfying properties analogous to the previously stated results concerning
\mathrm{Conv}^{\mathrm{Hecke}}
. In particular,
\mathrm{Conv}^{\mathrm{Hecke}}_{\mathcal{IW}}
sends simple objects to simple objects.

Proposition 8.5
.

The functor
\mathrm{Conv}^{\mathrm{Hecke}}
commutes with averaging functors. Namely, for any
\mathcal{S}\in\mathrm{mod}^{\mathbf{Y}}_{I_{\mathrm{u}}}(\mathcal{R})
we have a functorial isomorphism

\mathrm{Av}_{(I_{\mathrm{u}},\psi)}(\mathrm{Conv}^{\mathrm{Hecke}}(\mathcal{S}))\simeq\mathrm{Conv}^{\mathrm{Hecke}}_{\mathcal{IW}}(\mathrm{Av}_{(I_{\mathrm{u}},\psi)}(\mathcal{S})).

Proof.

First, one can easily check from the constructions of the averaging functors that, for any
\lambda\in\mathbf{Y}
, we have an isomorphism functorial in
\mathcal{S}\in\mathrm{Mod}^{\mathbf{Y}}_{I_{\mathrm{u}}}(\mathcal{R})
:

\mathrm{Av}_{(I_{\mathrm{u}},\psi)}(\mathcal{S}\star\mathcal{IC}^{\frac{\infty}{2}}_{\lambda})\simeq\mathrm{Av}_{(I_{\mathrm{u}},\psi)}(\mathcal{S})\star\mathcal{IC}^{\frac{\infty}{2}}_{\lambda}.

The conclusion then follows from the fact that
\mathrm{Av}_{(I_{\mathrm{u}},\psi)}
is an exact functor, so it preserves coequalizers.
∎

Lemma 8.6
.

The functor
\mathrm{Conv}^{\mathrm{Hecke}}
commutes with Verdier duality.

Proof.

Let
\mathcal{S}\in\mathrm{mod}^{\mathbf{Y}}_{I_{\mathrm{u}}}(\mathcal{R})
. Recall the construction of the Verdier duality on
\mathrm{mod}^{\mathbf{Y}}_{I_{\mathrm{u}}}(\mathcal{R})
from subsection
3.11
, and let
\alpha:\mathcal{S}_{1}\to\mathcal{S}_{2}
be a morphism between two free graded
\mathcal{R}
-modules such that
\mathcal{S}
is the cokernel of
\alpha
. By Remark
3.20
,
\mathbb{D}(\mathcal{S})
coincides with the kernel of
\mathbb{D}(\alpha):\mathbb{D}(\mathcal{S}_{2})\to\mathbb{D}(\mathcal{S}_{1})
. Let us spell out how
\mathbb{D}(\alpha)
was constructed. By additivity of
\mathbb{D}
, we may and will assume that
\mathcal{S}_{1}\simeq\Phi(\mathcal{F}_{1})
and
\mathcal{S}_{2}\simeq\Phi(\mathcal{F}_{2})\langle\mu\rangle
, for some
\mathcal{F}_{1},\mathcal{F}_{2}\in\mathrm{Perv}_{I_{\mathrm{u}}}(\mathrm{Gr})
, and
\mu\in\mathbf{Y}
. So (using Proposition
3.14
)
\alpha
comes from a map

\tilde{\alpha}:\mathcal{F}_{1}\to\mathcal{F}_{2}\star\mathcal{I}^{-w_{0}(\mu)+\nu}_{*}\star\mathcal{I}^{-w_{0}(\nu)}_{*}

in
\mathrm{Perv}_{I_{\mathrm{u}}}(\mathrm{Gr})
, for some large enough
\nu\in\mathbf{Y}^{+}\cap(w_{0}(\mu)+\mathbf{Y}^{+})
. Thus (up to taking a larger
\nu
),
\mathbb{D}(\alpha)
comes from the dual morphism

\beta:\mathbb{D}(\mathcal{F}_{2})\to\mathbb{D}(\mathcal{F}_{1})\star\mathcal{I}^{\mu-w_{0}(\nu)}_{*}\star\mathcal{I}^{\nu}_{*}.

Now, the object
\mathrm{Conv}^{\mathrm{Hecke}}(\mathbb{D}(\mathcal{S}))
is the kernel of
\mathrm{Conv}^{\mathrm{Hecke}}(\mathbb{D}(\alpha))
thanks to Proposition
8.3
.
By Proposition
8.1
, the latter morphism is a morphism between the objects
\mathbb{D}(\mathcal{F}_{2})\star\mathcal{IC}^{\frac{\infty}{2}}_{-\mu}
and
\mathbb{D}(\mathcal{F}_{1})\star\mathcal{IC}^{\frac{\infty}{2}}_{0}
. It is constructed as the composition

\displaystyle\mathbb{D}(\mathcal{F}_{2})\star\mathcal{IC}^{\frac{\infty}{2}}_{-\mu}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\beta\star\mathrm{id}}\mathbb{D}(\mathcal{F}_{1})\star(\mathcal{I}^{\mu-w_{0}(\nu)}_{*}\star\mathcal{I}^{\nu}_{*}\star\mathcal{IC}^{\frac{\infty}{2}}_{-\mu})

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\mathrm{id}\star H^{\prime}}\mathbb{D}(\mathcal{F}_{1})\star\mathcal{IC}^{\frac{\infty}{2}}_{0},

where
H^{\prime}
is the image of
H^{\nu}_{-\mu,0}
through the composition

\displaystyle\mathrm{Hom}((\mathcal{I}^{-w_{0}(\mu)+\nu}_{*}\star\mathcal{I}^{-w_{0}(\nu)}_{*})\star\mathcal{IC}^{\frac{\infty}{2}}_{0},\mathcal{IC}^{\frac{\infty}{2}}_{-\mu})

\displaystyle\xrightarrow[\sim]{\text{adjunction}}\mathrm{Hom}(\mathcal{IC}^{\frac{\infty}{2}}_{0},(\mathcal{I}^{\mu-w_{0}(\nu)}_{!}\star\mathcal{I}^{\nu}_{!})\star\mathcal{IC}^{\frac{\infty}{2}}_{-\mu})

\displaystyle\xrightarrow[\sim]{\mathbb{D}}\mathrm{Hom}((\mathcal{I}^{\mu-w_{0}(\nu)}_{*}\star\mathcal{I}^{\nu}_{*})\star\mathcal{IC}^{\frac{\infty}{2}}_{-\mu},\mathcal{IC}^{\frac{\infty}{2}}_{0}).

On the other hand,
\mathrm{Conv}^{\mathrm{Hecke}}(\mathcal{S})
is the cokernel of
\mathrm{Conv}^{\mathrm{Hecke}}(\alpha)
thanks to Proposition
8.3
. The latter morphism coincides with the composition

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{1}\star\mathcal{IC}^{\frac{\infty}{2}}_{0}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\tilde{\alpha}\star\mathrm{id}}\mathcal{F}_{2}\star(\mathcal{I}^{-w_{0}(\mu)+\nu}_{*}\star\mathcal{I}^{-w_{0}(\nu)}_{*}\star\mathcal{IC}^{\frac{\infty}{2}}_{0})

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\mathrm{id}\star H_{-\mu,0}^{\nu}}\mathcal{F}_{2}\star\mathcal{IC}^{\frac{\infty}{2}}_{-\mu}.

To conclude, we just need to notice that
\mathrm{Conv}^{\mathrm{Hecke}}(\mathbb{D}(\alpha))
and
\mathrm{Conv}^{\mathrm{Hecke}}(\alpha)
are transformed into one another by Verdier duality.
∎

Conjecture 8.1
.

The functor

\mathrm{Conv}^{\mathrm{Hecke}}:\mathrm{mod}^{\mathbf{Y}}_{I_{\mathrm{u}}}(\mathcal{R})\to\mathrm{Perv}_{{I_{\mathrm{u}}}}(\mathcal{F}l^{\frac{\infty}{2}})

induces an equivalence of categories between
\mathrm{mod}^{\mathbf{Y}}_{I_{\mathrm{u}}}(\mathcal{R})
and the subcategory of finite-length objects in
\mathrm{Perv}_{{I_{\mathrm{u}}}}(\mathcal{F}l^{\frac{\infty}{2}})
.

9.
Strategy of proof for Conjecture
8.1

We give a strategy of proof for Conjecture
8.1
, which follows
[
ABB+05
]
and will work provided Conjecture
6.1
holds.

9.1.
First steps

The strategy is to prove that
\mathrm{Conv}^{\mathrm{Hecke}}
respects the highest weight structures on both sides.

Lemma 9.1
.

Let
\gamma\in\mathbf{Y}^{+}
,
\nu\in\mathbf{Y}
and
w,w^{\prime}\in W
such that
\ell(ww_{0}w^{\prime})=\ell(w)+\ell(w_{0}w^{\prime})
. We have canonical isomorphisms

\mathcal{N}_{\gamma}\star^{I}\mathrm{Conv}^{\mathrm{Hecke}}(\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}^{\prime}_{\nu})\simeq\mathrm{Conv}^{\mathrm{Hecke}}(\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}^{\prime}_{\gamma+\nu}),\quad\mathcal{N}_{w}\star^{I}\mathrm{Conv}^{\mathrm{Hecke}}(\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}^{\prime}_{w^{\prime}t_{\nu}})\simeq\mathrm{Conv}^{\mathrm{Hecke}}(\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}^{\prime}_{ww^{\prime}t_{\nu}}).

Proof.

Recall that
\mathrm{Conv}^{\mathrm{Hecke}}(\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}^{\prime}_{\gamma+\nu})
represents the covariant functor which, to any
\mathcal{F}\in\mathrm{Ind}(\mathrm{Perv}_{{I_{\mathrm{u}}}}(\mathcal{F}l^{\frac{\infty}{2}}))
, associates the set of collections of morphisms

((\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}^{\prime}_{\gamma+\nu})_{\lambda}\star\mathcal{IC}^{\frac{\infty}{2}}_{-\lambda}\to\mathcal{F})_{\nu\in\mathbf{Y}}

such that, for any
\lambda,\mu\in\mathbf{Y}
, the following diagram commutes (cf. (
8.1
)):

(\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}^{\prime}_{\gamma+\nu})_{\lambda}\star\mathcal{R}_{-\mu}\star\mathcal{IC}^{\frac{\infty}{2}}_{\mu-\lambda}\rightrightarrows\mathcal{F}.

Applying Proposition
3.21
together with the first point of Lemma
3.4
, we obtain that
\mathrm{Conv}^{\mathrm{Hecke}}(\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}^{\prime}_{\gamma+\nu})
represents the covariant functor which, to any
\mathcal{F}\in\mathrm{Ind}(\mathrm{Perv}_{{I_{\mathrm{u}}}}(\mathcal{F}l^{\frac{\infty}{2}}))
, associates the set of collections of morphisms

((\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}^{\prime}_{\nu})_{\lambda}\star\mathcal{IC}^{\frac{\infty}{2}}_{-\lambda}\to\mathcal{D}_{-\gamma}\star\mathcal{F})_{\nu\in\mathbf{Y}}

such that, for any
\lambda,\mu\in\mathbf{Y}
, the following diagram commutes (cf. (
8.1
)):

(\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}^{\prime}_{\nu})_{\lambda}\star\mathcal{R}_{-\mu}\star\mathcal{IC}^{\frac{\infty}{2}}_{\mu-\lambda}\rightrightarrows\mathcal{D}_{-\gamma}\star\mathcal{F}.

By the first point of Lemma
3.4
again, this functor is represented by
\mathcal{N}_{\gamma}\star^{I}\mathrm{Conv}^{\mathrm{Hecke}}(\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}^{\prime}_{\nu})
.

The second isomorphism is proved in the same way, using that
\mathcal{N}_{w}\star^{I}\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}^{\prime}_{w^{\prime}t_{\nu}}\simeq\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}^{\prime}_{ww^{\prime}t_{\nu}}
(Proposition
3.22
).
∎

We have the following key result.

Proposition 9.2
.

Assume that Conjecture
6.1
holds. There is an isomorphism

(9.1)

\mathrm{Conv}^{\mathrm{Hecke}}(\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}^{\prime}_{0})\simeq\nabla_{0}^{\frac{\infty}{2}}.

9.2.
Proof of Proposition
9.2

Recall (cf. Proposition
3.23
) that, for any
w\in W_{\mathrm{ext}}
, the object
\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}_{w}
has a simple head, isomorphic to
\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{w}
. We start with a lemma (inspired by
[
ABB+05
, Corollary 4.4.14]
).

Lemma 9.3
.

Let
\nu\in\mathbf{Y}
.

(1)

The kernel of

f:\mathrm{Conv}^{\mathrm{Hecke}}(\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}_{w_{0}t_{\nu}}\to\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{w_{0}t_{\nu}})

is partially integrable.

(2)

For any
w\in W
,
\mathrm{IC}_{w_{0}t_{\nu}}^{\frac{\infty}{2}}
is the only non-partially integrable irreducible sub-quotient of
\mathrm{Conv}^{\mathrm{Hecke}}(\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}_{wt_{\nu}})
.

(3)

The object
\mathrm{IC}_{w_{0}t_{\nu}}^{\frac{\infty}{2}}
is the head of
\mathrm{Conv}^{\mathrm{Hecke}}(\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}^{\prime}_{\nu})
and the socle of
\mathrm{Conv}^{\mathrm{Hecke}}(\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}_{\nu})
. In particular,
\mathrm{Conv}^{\mathrm{Hecke}}(\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}^{\prime}_{\nu})
and
\mathrm{Conv}^{\mathrm{Hecke}}(\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}_{\nu})
are indecomposable.

Proof.

(1)

By exactness of the functor
\mathrm{Av}_{(I_{\mathrm{u}},\psi)}
(second point of Proposition
7.5
), it suffices to show that the image of
f
through
\mathrm{Av}_{(I_{\mathrm{u}},\psi)}
is an isomorphism. But we have

\mathrm{Av}_{(I_{\mathrm{u}},\psi)}(f)\simeq\mathrm{Conv}^{\mathrm{Hecke}}_{\mathcal{IW}}(\mathrm{Av}_{(I_{\mathrm{u}},\psi)}(\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}_{w_{0}t_{\nu}}\to\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{w_{0}t_{\nu}}))

thanks to Proposition
8.5
, and the morphism
\mathrm{Av}_{(I_{\mathrm{u}},\psi)}(\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}_{w_{0}t_{\nu}}\to\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{w_{0}t_{\nu}})
is easily seen to be an isomorphism thanks to Proposition
3.25
.

(2)

Let
w^{\prime}\in W
be such that
w=w^{\prime}w_{0}
. We have a canonical morphism

\mathcal{D}_{0}\to\mathcal{D}_{w^{\prime}}
, whose image through
\mathrm{Av}_{(I_{\mathrm{u}},\psi)}
is an isomorphism. Convolving it with
\mathrm{id}:\mathrm{Conv}^{\mathrm{Hecke}}(\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}_{w_{0}t_{\nu}})\to\mathrm{Conv}^{\mathrm{Hecke}}(\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}_{w_{0}t_{\nu}})
we obtain a morphism

\displaystyle\mathrm{Conv}^{\mathrm{Hecke}}(\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}_{w_{0}t_{\nu}})\to\mathcal{D}_{w^{\prime}}\star\mathrm{Conv}^{\mathrm{Hecke}}(\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}_{w_{0}t_{\nu}})

\displaystyle\overset{\text{Lem. \ref{lem commute convo}}}{\simeq}\mathrm{Conv}^{\mathrm{Hecke}}(\mathcal{D}_{w^{\prime}}\star\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}_{w_{0}t_{\nu}})

\displaystyle\overset{\text{Prop. \ref{prop convolution costandard 2}}}{\simeq}\mathrm{Conv}^{\mathrm{Hecke}}(\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}_{wt_{\nu}}).

The image of the above map through
\mathrm{Av}_{(I_{\mathrm{u}},\psi)}
is an isomorphism. So we conclude thanks to the first point and the second point of Proposition
7.5
.

(3)

Since Verdier duality swaps
\mathrm{Conv}^{\mathrm{Hecke}}(\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}^{\prime}_{\nu})
and
\mathrm{Conv}^{\mathrm{Hecke}}(\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}_{\nu})
(Lemma
8.6
) and preserves
\mathrm{IC}_{w_{0}t_{\nu}}^{\frac{\infty}{2}}
, it suffices to show that
\mathrm{IC}_{w_{0}t_{\nu}}^{\frac{\infty}{2}}
is the head of
\mathrm{Conv}^{\mathrm{Hecke}}(\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}^{\prime}_{\nu})
. In view of the second point, it suffices to show that, if
\mathrm{Conv}^{\mathrm{Hecke}}(\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}^{\prime}_{\nu})
maps to an irreducible object
\mathcal{S}
, then
\mathcal{S}
must be non-partially integrable. Here we repeat the argument of
[
ABB+05
, Proposition 2.3.2]
. So assume that
\mathrm{Conv}^{\mathrm{Hecke}}(\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}^{\prime}_{\nu})
maps to a partially integrable irreducible object
\mathcal{S}\in\mathrm{Perv}_{I_{\mathrm{u}}}(\mathcal{F}l^{\frac{\infty}{2}})
. Since
\mathcal{S}
is partially integrable and is an IC-sheaf, it must be equivariant with respect to a minimal parabolic subgroup
P_{s}\subset G
associated with a simple reflection
s
. Then
\mathcal{N}_{s}\star\mathcal{S}
lives in perverse cohomological degree
1
. However, we have an isomorphism

\mathcal{N}_{s}\star\mathrm{Conv}^{\mathrm{Hecke}}(\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}^{\prime}_{\nu})\simeq\mathrm{Conv}^{\mathrm{Hecke}}(\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}^{\prime}_{st_{\nu}})

by Proposition
3.22
, so that the above complex is perverse. Therefore

\mathrm{Hom}(\mathcal{N}_{s}\star\mathrm{Conv}^{\mathrm{Hecke}}(\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}^{\prime}_{\nu}),\mathcal{N}_{s}\star\mathcal{S})=0,

which is a contradiction since the functor
\mathcal{N}_{s}\star(-)
is an equivalence (with inverse
\mathcal{D}_{s}\star(-)
by the first point of Lemma
3.4
).

∎

We now construct a morphism

(9.2)

\varphi:\mathrm{Conv}^{\mathrm{Hecke}}(\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}^{\prime}_{0})\to\nabla^{\frac{\infty}{2}}_{0}.

Let
\mu\in\mathbf{Y}
. Recall (§
3.12
) that by
(\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}^{\prime}_{0})_{\mu}=\varinjlim_{\lambda\in\mathbf{Y}^{+}-\mu}\nabla_{\mu+\lambda}\star\mathcal{I}^{-w_{0}(\lambda)}_{*}.
For any
\lambda\in\mathbf{Y}^{+}-\mu
we define a morphism

\displaystyle\nabla_{\mu+\lambda}\star\mathcal{I}^{-w_{0}(\lambda)}_{*}\star\mathcal{IC}^{\frac{\infty}{2}}_{-\mu}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow[\sim]{\text{Prop. \ref{prop hecke geometric}}}\bigoplus_{\nu}\nabla_{\mu+\lambda}\star\mathcal{IC}^{\frac{\infty}{2}}_{\nu-\mu}\otimes\mathrm{Sat}(\mathcal{I}^{-w_{0}(\lambda)}_{*})(w_{0}(\nu))

\displaystyle\to\nabla_{\mu+\lambda}\star\mathcal{IC}^{\frac{\infty}{2}}_{-\mu-\lambda}\xrightarrow{\eqref{eq can}}\mathcal{N}_{\mu+\lambda}\star\nabla^{\frac{\infty}{2}}_{-\mu-\lambda}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow[\sim]{\text{Prop. \ref{Prop Action of convolution on standard objects}}}\nabla^{\frac{\infty}{2}}_{0}.

Thanks to the commutativity of the diagrams (
6.11
) and (
6.12
), the above morphisms are compatible with the transition morphisms in the limit defining
(\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}^{\prime}_{0})_{\mu}
. We therefore obtain a collection of morphisms
((\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}^{\prime}_{0})_{\mu}\star\mathcal{IC}^{\frac{\infty}{2}}_{-\mu}\to\nabla^{\frac{\infty}{2}}_{0})_{\mu\in\mathbf{Y}}
. The fact that this collection of morphisms factors through
\mathrm{Conv}^{\mathrm{Hecke}}(\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}^{\prime}_{0})
follows from the construction of the structures of right
\mathcal{R}
-module on
\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}^{\prime}_{0}
(§
3.12
) and left
\mathcal{R}
-module on
\mathrm{IC}^{\frac{\infty}{2}}
(§
6.6
). So we have defined a map
\varphi:\mathrm{Conv}^{\mathrm{Hecke}}(\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}^{\prime}_{0})\to\nabla^{\frac{\infty}{2}}_{0}
.

Proposition 9.4
.

The morphism
\mathrm{Av}_{(I_{\mathrm{u}},\psi)}(\varphi)
is an isomorphism.

Proof.

We have an isomorphism
\mathrm{Av}_{(I_{\mathrm{u}},\psi)}(\mathrm{Conv}^{\mathrm{Hecke}}(\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}^{\prime}_{0}))\simeq\mathrm{Conv}^{\mathrm{Hecke}}(\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}^{\prime\mathcal{IW}}_{0})
thanks to propositions
8.5
and
3.26
. But
\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}^{\prime\mathcal{IW}}_{0}\simeq\widehat{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathcal{IW}}_{0}
, so that we have an isomorphism

\mathrm{Av}_{(I_{\mathrm{u}},\psi)}(\mathrm{Conv}^{\mathrm{Hecke}}(\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}^{\prime}_{0}))\simeq\mathrm{IC}_{0}^{\psi}

by Corollary
8.2
. On the other hand, we also have an isomorphism

\mathrm{Av}_{(I_{\mathrm{u}},\psi)}(\nabla^{\frac{\infty}{2}}_{0})\overset{\text{Prop. \ref{prop averaging simples 2}}}{\simeq}\nabla^{\psi}_{0}\overset{\text{Prop. \ref{prop clean IC}}}{\simeq}\mathrm{IC}_{0}^{\psi}.

Therefore it suffices to check that
\mathrm{Av}_{(I_{\mathrm{u}},\psi)}(\varphi)
is nonzero to conclude. For that, it suffices to check that the image of

\nabla_{\mu+\lambda}\star\mathcal{IC}_{-\lambda-\mu}^{\frac{\infty}{2}}\to\mathcal{N}_{\mu+\lambda}\star\nabla^{\frac{\infty}{2}}_{-\mu-\lambda}\simeq\nabla^{\frac{\infty}{2}}_{0}

through
\mathrm{Av}_{(I_{\mathrm{u}},\psi)}
is nonzero for every
\mu\in\mathbf{Y},~\lambda\in\mathbf{Y}^{++}-\mu
, which brings back to checking that

\mathrm{Av}_{(I_{\mathrm{u}},\psi)}(\mathcal{N}_{\mu+\lambda}\star\mathrm{IC}_{-\lambda-\mu}^{\frac{\infty}{2}}\to\mathcal{N}_{\mu+\lambda}\star\nabla^{\frac{\infty}{2}}_{-\mu-\lambda})

is nonzero. But the latter is isomorphic to

\mathrm{Av}_{(I_{\mathrm{u}},\psi)}(\mathcal{N}_{\mu+\lambda})\star\mathrm{IC}_{-\lambda-\mu}^{\frac{\infty}{2}}\to\mathrm{Av}_{(I_{\mathrm{u}},\psi)}(\mathcal{N}_{\mu+\lambda})\star\nabla^{\frac{\infty}{2}}_{-\mu-\lambda},

where
\mathrm{Av}_{(I_{\mathrm{u}},\psi)}:\mathrm{Perv}_{I_{\mathrm{u}}}(\mathrm{Fl})\to\mathrm{Perv}_{\mathcal{IW}}(\mathrm{Fl})
is the averaging functor. The above morphism is obviously nonzero by construction of the convolution product.
∎

Next, we construct a morphism

\gamma:\nabla^{\frac{\infty}{2}}_{0}\to\mathrm{Conv}^{\mathrm{Hecke}}(\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}^{\prime}_{0}),

assuming that Conjecture
6.1
holds. Equivalently, we construct a morphism

\mathbb{D}(\gamma):\mathrm{Conv}^{\mathrm{Hecke}}(\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}_{0})\to\Delta^{\frac{\infty}{2}}_{0}.

By Corollary
3.30
, we need to construct (for all
\mu\in\mathbf{Y}
) the last arrow below

(9.3)

(\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}_{0})_{\mu}\star\mathcal{IC}^{\frac{\infty}{2}}_{-\mu}\simeq({{}^{w_{0}}(\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}^{\prime}_{w_{0}t_{-2\zeta}})})_{\mu}\star\mathcal{IC}^{\frac{\infty}{2}}_{-\mu}\to\Delta^{\frac{\infty}{2}}_{0}.

One can easily deduce from Proposition
3.21
that we have an isomorphism

({{}^{w_{0}}(\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}^{\prime}_{w_{0}t_{-2\zeta}})})_{\mu}\simeq\mathcal{N}_{w_{0}}\star^{I}({{}^{w_{0}}(\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}^{\prime}_{t_{-2\zeta}})})_{\mu}.

Thus, by the first point of Lemma
3.4
, constructing the morphism (
9.3
) is equivalent to constructing a morphism between
({{}^{w_{0}}(\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}^{\prime}_{t_{-2\zeta}})})_{\mu}\star\mathcal{IC}^{\frac{\infty}{2}}_{-\mu}
and
\mathcal{D}_{w_{0}}\star^{I}\Delta^{\frac{\infty}{2}}_{0}\simeq\Delta^{\frac{\infty}{2}}_{w_{0}}
. Now, recall that

({{}^{w_{0}}(\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}^{\prime}_{t_{-2\zeta}})})_{\mu}:=\varinjlim_{\lambda}\nabla_{w_{0}(\mu)+\lambda-2\zeta}\star\mathcal{I}^{-w_{0}(\lambda)}_{*}

and let us write
\delta:=w_{0}(\mu)+\lambda-2\zeta
for concision. We define a morphism

\displaystyle\nabla_{\delta}\star\mathcal{I}^{-w_{0}(\lambda)}_{*}\star\mathcal{IC}^{\frac{\infty}{2}}_{-\mu}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow[\sim]{\text{Prop. \ref{prop hecke geometric}}}\bigoplus_{\nu}\nabla_{\delta}\star\mathcal{IC}^{\frac{\infty}{2}}_{\nu-\mu}\otimes\mathrm{Sat}(\mathcal{I}^{-w_{0}(\lambda)}_{*})(w_{0}(\nu))

\displaystyle\to\nabla_{\delta}\star\mathcal{IC}^{\frac{\infty}{2}}_{-w_{0}(\lambda)-\mu}\xrightarrow{\text{Conj. \ref{conj commut diagram}}}\mathcal{N}_{\delta}\star\Delta^{\frac{\infty}{2}}_{w_{0}t_{-w_{0}(\lambda)-\mu+2\zeta}}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow[\sim]{\text{\eqref{eq standard times costandard}}}\Delta^{\frac{\infty}{2}}_{w_{0}}.

Thanks to the commutativity of the diagrams (
3.12
) and (
6.14
), the above morphisms are compatible with the transition morphisms in the limit defining
(\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}_{0})_{\mu}
. We therefore obtain a collection of morphisms
((\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}_{0})_{\mu}\star\mathcal{IC}^{\frac{\infty}{2}}_{-\mu}\to\Delta^{\frac{\infty}{2}}_{0})_{\mu\in\mathbf{Y}}
. Once again, the fact that this collection of morphisms factors through
\mathrm{Conv}^{\mathrm{Hecke}}(\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}_{0})
follows follows from the construction of the structures of right
\mathcal{R}
-module on
\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}^{\prime}_{0}
(§
3.12
) and left
\mathcal{R}
-module on
\mathcal{IC}^{\frac{\infty}{2}}
(§
6.6
).

Proposition 9.5
.

Assume that Conjecture
6.1
holds. The morphism
\varphi
is an isomorphism.

Proof.

First, notice that the same arguments as in the proof of Proposition
9.4
show that the morphism
\mathrm{Av}_{(I_{\mathrm{u}},\psi)}(\gamma)
is an isomorphism.

Thus, the morphism
\mathrm{Av}_{(I_{\mathrm{u}},\psi)}(\varphi\circ\gamma)\simeq\mathrm{Av}_{(I_{\mathrm{u}},\psi)}(\gamma)\circ\mathrm{Av}_{(I_{\mathrm{u}},\psi)}(\varphi)
is an isomorphism thanks to the previous claim and Proposition
9.4
. In particular, the composition

\nabla^{\frac{\infty}{2}}_{0}\xrightarrow{\gamma}\mathrm{Conv}^{\mathrm{Hecke}}(\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}^{\prime}_{0})\xrightarrow{\varphi}\nabla^{\frac{\infty}{2}}_{0}

is nonzero. Therefore
\varphi\circ\gamma
is the identity up to a nonzero scalar.

On the other hand, the morphism
\mathrm{Av}_{(I_{\mathrm{u}},\psi)}(\gamma\circ\varphi)\simeq\mathrm{Av}_{(I_{\mathrm{u}},\psi)}(\varphi)\circ\mathrm{Av}_{(I_{\mathrm{u}},\psi)}(\gamma)
is a nonzero endomorphism of

\mathrm{Av}_{(I_{\mathrm{u}},\psi)}(\mathrm{Conv}^{\mathrm{Hecke}}(\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}^{\prime}_{0}))\simeq\mathrm{Conv}^{\mathrm{Hecke}}_{\mathcal{IW}}(\mathrm{Av}_{(I_{\mathrm{u}},\psi)}(\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}^{\prime}_{0}))\simeq\mathrm{Conv}^{\mathrm{Hecke}}_{\mathcal{IW}}(\widehat{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathcal{IW}}_{0})\simeq\mathrm{IC}^{\psi}_{0},

so there exists
\alpha\in\mathbb{k}-\{0\}
such that
\mathrm{Av}_{(I_{\mathrm{u}},\psi)}(\alpha\cdot\gamma\circ\varphi)=\mathrm{id}
. Assume that
\gamma\circ\varphi
is not invertible. Thus
\alpha\cdot\gamma\circ\varphi
is not invertible. Since
\mathrm{Conv}^{\mathrm{Hecke}}(\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}^{\prime}_{0})
is indecomposable by the third point of Lemma
9.3
, its endomorphism ring is local. Therefore
\mathrm{id}-\alpha\cdot\gamma\circ\varphi
must be invertible. But

\mathrm{Av}_{(I_{\mathrm{u}},\psi)}(\mathrm{id}-\alpha\cdot\gamma\circ\varphi)=\mathrm{id}-\mathrm{Av}_{(I_{\mathrm{u}},\psi)}(\alpha\cdot\gamma\circ\varphi)=0,

which is a contradiction. So
\gamma\circ\varphi
is invertible.

From the two previous paragraphs we deduce that
\varphi
has both a left and a right inverse, so that it is invertible.
∎

9.3.
Proof of Conjecture
8.1
assuming Conjecture
6.1

Until the end, we assume that Conjecture
6.1
holds. We can then finally conclude.

Corollary 9.6
.

For all
w\in W_{\mathrm{est}}
, we have isomorphisms

\mathrm{Conv}^{\mathrm{Hecke}}(\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}^{\prime}_{w})\simeq\nabla^{\frac{\infty}{2}}_{w},\quad\mathrm{Conv}^{\mathrm{Hecke}}(\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}_{w})\simeq\Delta^{\frac{\infty}{2}}_{w}.

Proof.

By Lemma
8.6
and the fact that Verdier duality sends standard objects to costandard objects, it is enough to construct isomorphisms

\mathrm{Conv}^{\mathrm{Hecke}}(\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}^{\prime}_{w})\simeq\nabla^{\frac{\infty}{2}}_{w}

for all
w\in W_{\mathrm{ext}}
. First, if
w=t_{\lambda}
for some
\lambda\in\mathbf{Y}^{+}
, then the isomorphism is obtained from
\varphi
by convolution with
\mathcal{N}_{\lambda}
thanks to Lemma
9.1
and Proposition
6.7
.

Next, let
w:=xt_{\lambda}\in W_{\mathrm{ext}}
be an arbitrary element, and pick
\mu\in\mathbf{Y}^{+}
dominant enough so that
\lambda+\mu\in\mathbf{Y}^{+}
. Then we have

\mathcal{N}_{\mu}\star\mathrm{Conv}^{\mathrm{Hecke}}(\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}^{\prime}_{\lambda})\xrightarrow[\text{Lem. \ref{lem commute convo}}]{\sim}\mathrm{Conv}^{\mathrm{Hecke}}(\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}^{\prime}_{\lambda+\mu})\simeq\nabla^{\frac{\infty}{2}}_{\lambda+\mu},

where the last isomorphism follows from the first paragraph. Thanks to the first point of Lemma
3.4
, we can apply
\mathcal{D}_{-\mu}\star()
to obtain an isomorphism

\mathrm{Conv}^{\mathrm{Hecke}}(\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}^{\prime}_{\lambda})\simeq\mathcal{D}_{-\mu}\star\nabla^{\frac{\infty}{2}}_{\lambda+\mu}.

But thanks to Proposition
6.7
(and the first point of Lemma
3.4
), we know that

\mathcal{D}_{-\mu}\star\nabla^{\frac{\infty}{2}}_{\lambda+\mu}\simeq\nabla^{\frac{\infty}{2}}_{\lambda},

so that we have an isomorphism
\mathrm{Conv}^{\mathrm{Hecke}}(\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}^{\prime}_{\lambda})\simeq\nabla^{\frac{\infty}{2}}_{\lambda}
.

Finally, notice that we have isomorphisms
\mathcal{N}_{w_{0}}\star\mathrm{Conv}^{\mathrm{Hecke}}(\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}^{\prime}_{w_{0}t_{\lambda}})\simeq\mathrm{Conv}^{\mathrm{Hecke}}(\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}^{\prime}_{\lambda})
(Proposition
3.22
) and
\mathcal{N}_{w_{0}}\star\nabla^{\frac{\infty}{2}}_{w_{0}t_{\lambda}}\simeq\nabla^{\frac{\infty}{2}}_{\lambda}
(equation (
5.5
)). Thanks to the first point of Lemma
3.4
, we deduce that there exists an isomorphism
\mathrm{Conv}^{\mathrm{Hecke}}(\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}^{\prime}_{w_{0}t_{\lambda}})\simeq\nabla^{\frac{\infty}{2}}_{w_{0}t_{\lambda}}
. Applying the functor
\mathcal{N}_{xw_{0}}\star()
to the latter isomorphism, we obtain the desired isomorphism thanks to Proposition
3.22
and equation (
5.5
).
∎

We can conclude the proof of Theorem
8.1
. Combining the results of the two previous subsections, we obtain that the functor
\mathrm{Conv}^{\mathrm{Hecke}}
is exact and satisfies

\mathrm{Conv}^{\mathrm{Hecke}}(\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}_{w})\simeq\Delta^{\frac{\infty}{2}}_{w},~\mathrm{Conv}^{\mathrm{Hecke}}(\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}^{\prime}_{w})\simeq\nabla^{\frac{\infty}{2}}_{w},~\mathrm{Conv}^{\mathrm{Hecke}}(\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{w})\simeq\mathrm{IC}^{\frac{\infty}{2}}_{w}

for all
w\in W_{\mathrm{ext}}
. Since both categories
\mathrm{mod}^{\mathbf{Y}}_{I_{\mathrm{u}}}(\mathcal{R})
and
\mathrm{Perv}_{{I_{\mathrm{u}}}}(\mathcal{F}l^{\frac{\infty}{2}})
are Artinian and satisfy the Ext vanishing property between standard and costandard objects (cf. Proposition
5.17
and Proposition
3.24
), we can apply
[
ABB+05
, Lemma 6.2.7]
to conclude that
\mathrm{Conv}^{\mathrm{Hecke}}
is an equivalence of categories.
